# The Universe Bible — Phase 2 companion: chapter content maps, open-items sweep, glossary draft

**Date:** 2026-09-24
**Status:** Working material for Phases 3–4. Nothing here is true until baked into the bible; nothing here changes canon.
**What it is:** the raw material the bible's chapters are written from. One section per chapter (worlds; beings; growth; story; roles, governance, community and products): A. what is settled and where it is said (present tense, with cites) · B. rulings needed (the master list is `record--universe-bible-phase-2-rulings.md`; these are the per-chapter originals) · C. candidates touching the chapter · D. open items · E. glossary terms. Then the open-items sweep (75 distinct items, O-001–O-075, and what the sweep proposes to close or drop). Then the glossary draft assembled from the chapters.
**Cites:** S<n> = statement in `discovery--the-universe-in-the-making.md`; canon §n = section of the named canon file; T<line> = a Session 01 reconciliation tag by line number; CB-x = Candidate B item; ADR-Unnn as usual.

---



# Chapter map — the worlds

Sources read: `canon--cosmology--worlds-topology.md` (all), Session 01 S10–S12, S17–S22, S25–S27, S31–S48 and its 2026-09-17 tag layer, Session 02 S49–S65, Session 03 S66–S78 (S59-related and the time-axis threads), Candidates A, B and C (Part 2), ADR-U025, ADR-U031, `questions--` (CQ-010, CQ-014, CQ-018), `record--universe-to-spec-manifestation.md`, VISION.md "Structural concepts", the growth core's engagement spectrum, and the two research reports (the "Whisp cosmology" section, the "Three-Level Shadow" synthesis, Kegan §4.2–4.4). Nothing below is canon; precedence per the brief (Session 03 > 02 > 01 > canon files > candidates; Statement over Held; ADRs and constitutional docs bind).

---

### A. Settled — what the bible can state as true

#### A.1 The shape of the universe

- FringeIsland is the name of the whole universe — everything past the Shimmer, both worlds and all three reaches — and of the enterprise that makes it. [S63]
- The Ordinary World is daily life, unmodified; the Shimmer is one membrane between it and both worlds behind it. [opening frame, S31, S49]
- Behind the Shimmer lie two worlds, overlaid on the same Ordinary-World coordinates: place 2 (warm, welcoming; name pending) and Marath (place 3, hostile). [S31, S50, S63]
- "The Fringe" is the inhabited band beside the edge — the whole created world on the far side of the Shimmer; an edge is a line, a fringe is the band beside it. [S26, cosmology §2–3]
- The Void is not a place on this map but an axis across it: the fact that the Whisp has left the FIM and hangs on the cord. [S27, cosmology §5]

#### A.2 The Ordinary World

- Where we live, work and engage with other humans — our ordinary days. [opening frame, S26]
- It looks exactly as everyone experiences it in everyday life. [S49]
- It is the substrate the two near sides overlay: same coordinates, different perceptual access. [S10, S31, S32]
- The FIM's body never leaves the Ordinary World and never enters danger; the FIM journeys "mentally", with the Whisp, while staying safe. [S22, S27]
- Body-safe does not mean body-still: moving the body changes the angle on the Shimmer and which near side is visible from there. The body is a roving vantage into the near side. [S27]
- The FIM's ball (the home gateway) is always one step away beside the FIM in the Ordinary World. [S33, S42]

#### A.3 The Shimmer

- The membrane / divide between the Ordinary World and the Fringe. [S26, S49]
- There is one Shimmer; both worlds lie behind it — not one Shimmer per world. [S31, S49]
- The Shimmer is a line, not a band (the edge); the Fringe is what grows beside it. [S26]
- The Gimbal — the senses surface — opens the Shimmer to reveal the near side of both worlds; things and creatures of both worlds can be seen as part of the Ordinary World only by looking through it. [S52, ADR-U025]
- The Whisp is the faculty of perception, the Gimbal the device, the Shimmer the thing perceived. [S10, S26]
- Perception through the Shimmer scales with how full the FIM's Whisp is — with self-disclosure and the senses. [S17–S18, S26]
- The world is built so that its full experience requires all five senses; a FIM whose Whisp is under-filled perceives only part of it; two FIMs at the same field can have sensorily different experiences of it. [S18]
- The Shimmer is a boundary between worlds; the Void is a different axis. A Whisp pointed at a field in the Ordinary World is already in the Void and has not yet crossed the Shimmer. [S27]

#### A.4 The two worlds: place 2 (name pending) and Marath

- Place 2 is the warm, welcoming world, the flourishing the universe is for. It resonates with the Whisp, the growth inner voice. [S31, S50, S51]
- Place 2 is a whole world, not an island: an island may be in it, and much more. [S64]
- Place 2's name is pending: it is to be derived from the Whisp's voice, as Marath derives from Mara, by a discovery journey; Stefan names it. [S63, S65]
- Place 2 is not a comfort zone: only each FIM's own ball is inviolable; everything else in it is tendable and can be wounded. The name should carry warmth first, challenge second. [S32, S34, S63]
- Marath is place 3: hostile, dystopian, "at first sight evil" — the counterpart of place 2. [S50]
- Marath is the manifestation of Mara, the negative inner voice that people listen to without knowing it. Place 2 resonates with the Whisp; Marath resonates with Mara. [S51]
- The two worlds are the warm and hostile centres of gravity of one continuous tonal spectrum; the "all in between" lives in the contested ground between them. [S12, S31]
- "Positive future / negative future" is the stake, not a coordinate: Marath is the force that would unmake the bright future, not a timeline one travels to. [S31] (See B.4–B.6 for the Session 03 reopening.)
- Place 2 is revived Marath: the same ground in two states, alive/glowing (tended) versus dead/black (un-grown or receded). [S35]
- The Shadow is what Mara looks like when met in Marath; there is one Shadow per FIM. [S53]
- Marath holds other creatures besides Shadows, and things happen there in which more than one's own Shadow plays a role; a FIM meets other FIMs' Shadows there. [S53]
- Another FIM's Shadow manifests to you only along a shared branch; what you see is its form and force, never its content; content only by the FIM's own disclosure. [S62]
- The confrontation with Mara is always mediated through the Whisp: the Whisp walks into Marath and meets the Shadow; the FIM stays body-side. That is the point, not an accident. [S61, S27]
- Marath is where a FIM's Big Assumptions are tested; the Shadow is the immune system met from outside oneself. [S56]
- Marath's near side has authored inhabitants (androids guarding a dirt road under a grey sky). [S32, S15]

#### A.5 The near-side coupling

- The two near sides are the same Ordinary-World coordinates seen two ways: the road with warm traffic in place 2 is an overgrown dirt track under androids in Marath, at the same spot. [S32]
- The two readings are causally coupled: the patch eating flowers in place 2 and the machine drilling in Marath are the same wound seen from both sides. [S32]
- To heal a wound in place 2, a FIM traces it and acts on its cause in Marath — notice the blight, cross a portal, stop the cause, watch the warm world heal. [S32]
- In its deeper meaning, healing a wound in place 2 by acting on its cause in Marath is confronting one's own negative self-talk at its source. [S51]
- The coupling is the glowing ground reverting to black at a co-located point. [S35]

#### A.6 The three reaches: near side, far side, beyond

- Each world has three reaches: a near side, a far side and a beyond. [S52]
- Near side: the world manifested as a layer on top of the Ordinary World, seen through the Shimmer; coordinate-tied; the FIM's body is the anchor; moving the body moves the view. [S27, S45, S52]
- Far side: away from the Ordinary World; no coordinates in it correspond to earthly coordinates. [S52]
- What the canon core calls "the Beyond" is the far side; it is renamed at ratification. The village and the private home are on the far side of place 2. "Into place 3 (the deep dark)" is Marath's far side. [S55, S33]
- The beyond is even further away and not yet known. It currently contains nothing named — still to be discovered, or kept unmapped on purpose for curiosity. [S52, S55]
- Marath's beyond is a place you really do not want to go near. [S52]
- The Shadow's size grows with depth into Marath: small on the near side (glimpsed in the everyday through the Gimbal), proportionate on the far side, and Marath's beyond is the Shadow at full strength — Mara undiluted. [S59, confirmed in use by S60]
- The beyond's danger is relative to the FIM, not absolute: unapproachable while Mara is full, approachable as it empties. It may be the last thing a FIM meets before graduation. [S60]
- The beyond is optional: the road there runs through every immunity the FIM carries, so many FIMs will not want to go and for many it will not be possible. Graduation is not gated on it; FringeIsland has no end to reach. [S60]
- Whether the anchor ladder has a fourth rung for the beyond, or the beyond cannot be reached by anchoring at all, is an unsolved mystery, kept so. [S55]
- The beyond wants a capitalised name of its own, to be discovered later. [S55]
- Curiosity is a design stance: some of the universe stays unmapped; something must itch to be found interesting. [S55, S70]

#### A.7 The Void and the cord

- The Void is an axis, not a region: it is wherever the Whisp is once it leaves the FIM — from just outside the skull, along the whole cord, to wherever the Whisp journeys, in the Ordinary World as well as beyond the Shimmer. [S27]
- The other worlds say where the Whisp travelled; the Void is the fact that it left and hangs exposed on the lifeline. [S27]
- The Void is also the primordial black nothingness that Marath expresses and place 2 is revived from; everything in place 2 except the ball can recede toward the Void / Marath if untended. [S34, S35]
- The cord is the FIM–Whisp lifeline; it runs through the Void for its whole length and is exposed the whole way. [S27]
- If the cord is cut the Whisp suffers and may die; never the FIM (the Jake / Avatar structure). Harm travels the bond toward the Whisp, never back to the FIM. [S22, S27, S48]
- The cord runs from the FIM (origin) to the Whisp (far end); the FIM decides how long it may be — pay out to venture, reel in to retreat. The cord is the FIM's dial. [S40]
- Cord length is developmental intimacy: short = held close, long = trusted into distance. The voluntariness dial and the intimacy dial are the same dial. [S48]
- Two independent levers set risk: how far you go (length) and how well you protect that distance (seeds); risk is the product; the peril is opt-in. [S40]
- The cord carries two signals on two channels: maturity owns salience (vivid early, receding as the Whisp is internalised); health owns colour and integrity (whole vs frayed, steady vs flickering, warm vs cold). [S27, S37]
- A friend perceives your cord's health only along a branch grown with you; glanceable not diagnostic, invited not imposed, self first (your own Whisp is the first responder). [S37, S38]
- The cord exists from the Mist's first moment, unpaid-out (near-zero length, the Whisp "in the skull"); metamorphosis is its first paying-out, made possible by the ball as anchor-root; the cord is kept, not severed — FIM and Whisp forever two-but-bound. [S39, S48, ADR-U031]
- The cord (FIM to own Whisp) and the branch (FIM to FIM) are two distinct bonds, never collapsed: losing a branch thins the world there; it never kills a Whisp. [S36]

#### A.8 Anchoring, seeds and portals

- The gate between reaches is anchoring, not safe-versus-dangerous. The anchor ladder runs body → ball → disposable seeds. [S45]
- Near side (both worlds): coupled to Ordinary-World coordinates, so the body is the anchor; no seed; Mist-open. [S45]
- Far side of place 2 (the village, the private home): the ball is the permanent anchor-root, reached through the always-on home gateway; no fresh seed; FIM-only. [S45, S39]
- Into Marath (the deep dark): fresh seeds budded from the ball, planted along the route; FIM-only. [S45, S40]
- Only the near side is anchor-free, because reality itself holds you there. [S45]
- Anchoring is a concrete act — fastening the cord to objects, like a climber clipping protection along a route — and the FIM and Whisp anchor many times along a route. [S27, S40]
- The anchor objects are seeds budded from the FIM's home ball. [S40]
- A seed is at once climbing-protection and a patch of place-2 life reclaimed from the dead ground: anchoring and growing the world are the same gesture; world-genesis at small scale. [S40, S35]
- The dangerous far reach is reachable directly from the Ordinary World through field portals: the cord runs from where the FIM physically stands, into the portal, and out into Marath by the Whisp. [S40]
- Thresholds are doors, passages, rabbit holes — discrete crossings, as distinct from the Shimmer, which is a membrane. [S11, S26]
- Seed-to-pass: a danger-portal requires a seed dropped on its edge to cross. The threshold seed is the route's anchor-zero and the marked way back to the Ordinary World. [S40]
- The safe crossings — the home gateway and the village — need no seed. Because seeds need a ball, the rule hard-locks Mists out of every far reach. [S40, S45]
- The ball absorbs the home portal: the ball is the gateway. Pressing its inside transports the FIM to the private home; pressing its outer rim transports them to the village. Centre = inward/private, edge = outward/social. [S33, S42]
- The ball's visualisation exists on both the Gimbal and the Hub; it needs no sensors, so it appears on both equipment profiles. [S42, ADR-U025]

#### A.9 Severance, stuck cords and rescue

- Severance recovery is two-tier: anchored — fall back to the last anchor and resume; unanchored overreach — start over from the very beginning. [S27]
- Recovery is reunion: the FIM and Whisp retreat and find each other again before continuing. [S27]
- If a portal closes: a destroyed threshold seed leaves the cord dead (the severe tier); a living seed leaves it stuck — length frozen, the Whisp moves only within its slack, cannot extend or withdraw through the shut door. [S40]
- Stuck is not lost: the door can be reopened, and a friend can rescue along a branch — reopen the door or re-anchor. Stuck is the one failure where the dial jams. [S40]
- The human is never the thing stuck: the FIM's body stays in the Ordinary World with the gateway beside it and can always withdraw to safety, at the cost of abandoning the venture. [S40, S22, S33]
- FIMs strengthen and heal each other's cords — only along a branch they have grown together; the branch is the channel through which one FIM steadies another's lifeline. [S27, S36]
- The two-tier severance and the nested respawn of the narrative (home base within an episode; episode-repeat) are one system: severance is the cause, anchoring the currency. [S21, S27]
- Anchoring is a discipline that encodes a growth lesson: consolidate before advancing; venturing far without anchoring is panic-zone overshoot with a concrete consequence. [S27]

#### A.10 The village, the Tree, the balls, the branches, the private home

- The village (working name) is the safe-harbour commons: the bursting little sea-village where FIMs relax, chill, contemplate, socialise, and just be. [S26, S41]
- The village sits on the far side of place 2; it is one place within that world, possibly an island; the world holds much more. [S33, S55, S64]
- The village is FIM-only: a Mist has no ball, hence no anchor on any far side. [S45]
- In the village stands the Tree, hung with glowing glass balls. Each ball is one FIM, the entrance to their private place; the Tree is the official portal between the commons and all private places. [S26]
- The ball is granted only at metamorphosis (all founding questions answered AND consent given); it exists only on the far side of consent. [S39, S47, ADR-U031]
- All balls glow equal: no one is better or worse than anyone else; the variable signals live on the cord and the branch, never on the ball. Ball-brightness-as-fullness was considered and paused. [S37]
- The ball is the home base, the seed-source and the anchor-root: it gives the cord purchase on the far world. [S39]
- Each FIM's own ball is the one inviolable thing in place 2. [S34]
- The branches are the FIM-to-FIM bonds and they are what the Tree is made of: the social graph is not drawn on the Tree, it is the Tree. [S35, S38]
- Own branches are legible; the wider crown stays ambient — no rankings, no counts. A 1+1 branch is a single bough; a 1+community bond is a braid where several balls' branches gather. [S38]
- Branch glow is the relationship's aliveness: a tended bond glows strong; a drifted one thins and waits, recoverable when tended again. [S38]
- Branch brightness gates how much of a friend's cord-health you can perceive and help heal. [S37, S38]
- Following your own branches is how you find friends among the balls; a branch is the visible route to a friend's ball. A stranger's ball stays invitation-gated. [S26, S38]
- The private home is a self-chosen representation of where the FIM feels safest — a flower field with pathways, an attic den somewhere unknown, a house on a trunk above the clouds; a place to be creative and feel free. [S43, S26]
- The home is inviolable: default-locked, the FIM holds the only key; the FIM can selectively invite other FIMs to the whole home or parts of it, granted and revocable. [S26, S43]
- The home is furnished with the personal-scope slice of World Studio, open to every FIM; it evolves as the FIM grows and the Whisp fills. [S44]
- The commons and the private home stay distinct: the village is collective, the home is private. [S26]

#### A.11 World-genesis: gardening, not guarding

- The warm world is not pre-built and defended; it is grown. Balls rise from the black nothingness ground; their bonds weave into branches and form the crown; the tree grows and brings the dead ground to life, which glows and spreads → village → island → all of place 2. [S35]
- The causal order is what holds (balls and bonds first, everything precipitating from them; the Void primordial; the warm world = revived Void); "up versus down" is an aesthetic choice held loosely. [S35]
- The world is inherited: a newcomer arrives into a crown earlier FIMs grew and adds their own ball and branches. [S35]
- Only the FIM's own ball is inviolable; the rest of place 2 — commons, village, ground — can recede toward the Void / Marath if untended. [S34]
- The governing rule: gardening, not guarding. Tending must feel like a beloved garden — gentle, forgiving, approach-motivated — never like defending a fort. [S34]
- Nothing is permanently destroyed; the worst case is "not yet grown" or "receded", recoverable the moment care resumes. A FIM who steps away returns to a world overgrown and waiting, not shrunken and punished. [S34, S19]
- The world's objects and artifacts are continuously made by contributing FIMs (Dreamineers and Creators); it is not pre-built and static. [S13]
- Grow is delight, not deficiency — the value the gardening rule protects. [S28, S34]

#### A.12 The growth gradient, the zones, and depth into Marath

- The comfort → growth → panic gradient is spatial. [S26]
- The distance that matters is Void distance — how far the Whisp has ventured out on the cord — not bodily distance. You can stand still and send your Whisp deep, or walk a mile with your Whisp close. "Far from the safe harbour" means far out on the lifeline. [S27]
- Danger is pervasive along the cord, not localised to a threshold: the far reaches are dangerous because more lifeline is exposed, not because "the far side is scary". [S27]
- Venture out for challenge, return home for safety: the developmental breath-rhythm rendered as geography, meta-safe because the human is never the thing at risk. [S19, S22, S26]
- The zones are comfort → fear → learning → growth, with panic past the fence. They are the felt experience of the Mara-to-Whisp transfer, informed by Immunity to Change (informed by, never equal to). [S73, S74]
- Fear is where Mara speaks loudest and is met as the Shadow, through the Whisp; learning is where the test is run in Marath under meta-safety and respawn; growth is where Mara's authority has emptied into the Whisp. [S74]
- The Whisp never opens the panic zone, even when a FIM asks for more depth: a test that overwhelms confirms the Big Assumption and strengthens the immunity. Voluntariness sets the ceiling; care sets the floor. [S19, S58, S74]
- You do not enter the panic zone before you have a home to return to; the Mist lock on the far reaches is intrinsic, not a fence. [S39, S45]
- The zone bands sit on the existing Void-distance gradient alongside the Shadow's growth with depth. [S59, S74]
- The FIM dials the depth of the invitation — how much of Mara is surfaced and tested — never the outcome; ITC scales from one small assumption up to a move between orders, if the FIM wishes and allows it. [S58]
- The dark future is the stake of the universe and the basis of its storytelling; it never drives an individual FIM's pace, depth or cord dial, and is never used to hurry or guilt a FIM. [S70, S73]
- The world is not designed to frighten: fear is Mara's voice met through the Whisp, not a feeling the world is built to cause. [S70, S73, S74]

#### A.13 Who can be where

- Mist: has its own Whisp and cord from the first moment (cord unpaid-out); full near-side access, body-anchored; no ball, so no far side (no village, no private home) and no Marath depths; the lock is intrinsic — no ball, no purchase — not a permission fence. Its presence is session-ephemeral and unlinkable. [S39, S45, S47, S48, ADR-U031]
- FIM: near side of both worlds; far side of place 2 through the ball; Marath's depths through seeds at chosen risk; the beyond — unsolved. [S45, S55]
- Feature gating follows equipment plus status: AR needs sensors; the ball gates the far reaches. [ADR-U025, ADR-U031]
- NPCs act in the world alongside FIMs; Marath has creatures of its own. [S15, S32, S53]

#### A.14 What is retired (mapping for old text)

- The Three Worlds model (Ordinary World / Safe Harbour / The Other Side) is superseded: the Safe Harbour is the village; the Other Side is the Fringe's two worlds; the Void "between worlds" with "three dimensions" is superseded by the Void as axis. [cosmology "What this supersedes", growth core note]
- "Overlay / overlaid register" → near side; "beyond register" → far side. [S26]
- "FringeIsland-the-island / the FringeIsland place" as place 2 → place 2 (name pending); "FringeIsland" is the universe. [S63, S64]
- The collective safe harbour as "always safe" → only the FIM's own ball is inviolable; the commons is tendable. [S34]
- "Distance from the safe harbour" as the gradient → Void distance on the cord. [S27]
- "Place 3 stays unnamed" → Marath. [S50]
- "Shadow" as the anonymous entrant → the Mist; "Shadow" is the place-3 menace, now Mara's form. [S47, S53, ADR-U031]

### B. Rulings needed — contradictions, stale text, ambiguities

1. **"The Beyond" collides three ways.** The canon core (§4, §6, §11, anchor table, "What this supersedes"), VISION.md "Structural concepts", the roles core ("no ball, so no Beyond"), ADR-U031 ("the Beyond (the village, deep place 3)") and the growth core's engagement spectrum (Homebody / Explorer / **Beyond** — "past the village and far out on the cord") all use "Beyond" for what S52/S55 now call the far side. S55 rules the rename and says the new furthest reach "probably wants a capitalised name of its own — to be discovered later". *Suggested ruling:* Option 1 — the bible writes "far side" everywhere the old text says "Beyond", writes the third reach as lowercase "the beyond" with an explicit "name pending" note, and never reuses the capitalised "Beyond" for the new reach (old text would silently mean the wrong thing). Also rename the growth core's third engagement pole (e.g. "Voyager") or state in the growth chapter that it is an engagement mode, not the reach — a reader will otherwise read it as "FIMs who go to the beyond", which S60 says almost no one does. Option 2 — give the new reach the capital "the Beyond" and accept the collision with every pre-Session-02 document. Why: Option 1 is what S55 says and avoids a two-meaning word in the one document meant to be a single truth.

2. **What "FringeIsland" and "the Fringe" each name after S63.** S63 lifts "FringeIsland" to the universe and the enterprise. S26 already had "the Fringe" as "the whole created universe on the far side of the Shimmer" and "FringeIsland Universe encapsulates everything from directly on top of the Ordinary World all the way to the far Beyond". S50 ("Place 2 is FringeIsland") and the canon core §3 ("the FringeIsland place") are superseded. Unsaid: whether "the Fringe" survives as a term and whether it is the same thing as the FringeIsland universe. *Suggested ruling:* the Fringe = the in-world name for everything past the Shimmer (both worlds, all reaches); FringeIsland = the universe seen from outside (the project, the product, the whole including the Ordinary-World frame); they overlap almost entirely and the bible says so once. Why: S26's edge/fringe distinction is load-bearing (the project is named for the band), and dropping "the Fringe" would orphan it.

3. **Place 2 has no name; the bible needs a way to write it.** S63/S65 decide the method (derive from the Whisp's voice, Stefan names); Candidate C ran twelve passes without a lock, and its fourth pass raised the still-open question whether the cosy brief describes the world or only the village/home. *Suggested ruling:* write "place 2 (name pending)" in the bible, with the naming journey marked Open; and rule the world-or-village question separately (see B.9). Why: a placeholder name would leak into specs and the novel exactly as "FringeIsland-the-island" did.

4. **Does the cosmology have a time axis?** S31 (Session 01) settled "positive/negative future" as stake, not coordinate; the canon core §3 says so. S66 reopens it ("two ways the future can take") and S67/S71 lock that the Whisp is the FIM's own future whole self reaching back, a possible not guaranteed future. S66 challenge 7 and S59 challenge 4 ask whether place 2's beyond *is* the future or the source of the Whisp's wisdom; Session 03's open threads list "whether time is an axis of the cosmology at all" as unsaid. *Suggested ruling:* Option 1 — the topology has no time axis; the future is the stake carried in the backdrop (S67, S71) and reaches the FIM through the Whisp, not through any place; the beyond of either world is not "the future". Option 2 — place 2's beyond is where the future self reaches from (the source of the Whisp's wisdom) and Marath's beyond is the collapse at full strength, making the two beyonds the two futures. Why Option 1 as default: it is what S31 and the canon say and needs no new mechanics; Option 2 is Claude's pattern (S59 ch.4, S66 b) that Stefan never confirmed — if he wants it, it is a statement, not a ruling.

5. **Marath as the dark future at civilisational scale.** S66 connection (b) and S70 challenge (2) propose Marath = Mara at civilisational scale, its beyond = the catastrophe future. Offered by Claude, listed by Session 03 as "not confirmed by Stefan". S51 (Marath resonates with Mara) is the only locked link. *Suggested ruling:* keep Open; the bible states S51 and no more. Why: it changes what Marath's beyond *is* (S59: Mara at full strength *for that FIM*, S60: relative to the FIM) from personal to collective, which S60's "relative to the FIM" does not obviously survive.

6. **Fork or continuum.** S66 says "two ways the future can take"; S31 reconciled the two places as two poles of one continuum; S66 challenge 6 says the same probably holds for the future — unanswered. *Suggested ruling:* continuum, consistent with S31 and with S60 (the beyond is a gradient the FIM may or may not walk); the bible avoids "fork" language. Why: nothing in S67–S72 needs a binary.

7. **The Shadow: stale canon text and the "class" hedge.** Canon §3 says "the full nature of the Shadow — force, entity, or class — is open" and calls it the "place-3 / sleep-paralysis menace" (from S47, ADR-U031). S53 settles it: the Shadow is Mara's form when met in Marath, one per FIM; "maybe" also the class of Marath's creatures; Marath has other creatures. *Suggested ruling:* the bible states S53's firm half (Mara's form, one per FIM, other creatures exist) and marks "class of all Marath creatures" Open; "sleep paralysis" becomes a lineage note (the Scandinavian *mara*, the night-spirit of sleep paralysis — S51 ch.4) rather than a description of the menace. Why: S53 is later and Stefan's; the "sleep-paralysis" tag was never a mechanic.

8. **Candidate B item E against Session 02 — where sleep paralysis lives.** Item E maps lucid dreaming → place 2, out-of-body → place 3, sleep paralysis → "caught in the Shimmer itself" (a third mode: held, not released; the classic "presence" belongs to the membrane, "not to place 2 or place 3"), and says the same crossing lands in place 2 or 3 depending on inner state ("the Fringe reflects something back"). Session 02 places the sleep-paralysis figure in Marath: S47/ADR-U031 reserve "Shadow" for the place-3 menace, S51 ch.4 ties the name Mara to the sleep-paralysis *mara*, S53 makes the Shadow Mara's form met in Marath. Two further conflicts: (a) S32/S52 have both near sides visible at once through the Gimbal and entry into Marath's depths by a chosen, seeded portal (S40) — not by an inner state that decides which face receives you; (b) "the ball as key that releases you from the Shimmer" is not in any statement. *Suggested ruling:* drop item E's mapping as mechanics. If Stefan wants a "held in the Shimmer" failure state (a crossing that neither completes nor rejects), it would be a new statement in the fundamentals session; otherwise the three altered states stay lineage for the Gimbal's origin (item A), not topology. Why: three separate Session 02 statements contradict it and it was never promoted.

9. **Candidate B item E (out-of-body → Marath) against S61.** Item E's out-of-body reading has the FIM "observing from outside yourself" in place 3. S27/S61 lock that the FIM never leaves the body; the Whisp is the one out, and the confrontation with Mara is always through the Whisp — "that is probably the point". *Suggested ruling:* drop the place-3 mapping; at most, "out-of-body" describes the FIM's felt experience of the Whisp far out on the cord (the Void, any world), which S27 already carries as "experiential co-presence". Why: S61 is later, Stefan's, and explicit about intent.

10. **Which crossings need a seed — Marath's near side, and place 2's far side outside the village.** Three statements pull differently. S45 (Stefan): "Portals to the far side of place 2 do need seeds"; its Held resolution makes the near side of *both* worlds body-anchored, seed-free and Mist-open, and the village/home ball-anchored — but says nothing about far-side-of-place-2 destinations that are not the village or home, and its own correction bullet says a Mist "is confined to the near-side warm overlay" (place 2 only). S32: to stop the drilling at the *same coordinates* the FIM "crosses a portal into place 3" — i.e. a portal into Marath's *near side*. S40: field portals from the Ordinary World "out to the dangerous space 3" need a threshold seed. S52: the Gimbal reveals *both* near sides. *Suggested ruling:* (a) Near side of Marath: seeing it needs only the Gimbal; acting in it (crossing the coupling to reach a cause) is a near-side crossing that needs no seed because the body still anchors — so Mists can perceive Marath's near side and its creatures, which is what S52 says ("things and creatures from FringeIsland and Marath can be seen"); whether a Mist may *act* there is Open. (b) Far reaches of either world not reached through the ball (the wild reaches of place 2 past the village; all of Marath's far side) are seed-anchored, per S45's own words. Why: it keeps "only the near side is anchor-free" (S45) literally true, and makes the anchor ladder about reaches, not about worlds — which is what S45 says the gate is.

11. **The Shadow's size — Statement vs Held.** S59 is a question from Stefan ("does the size correlate with how far…? The beyond might be the full strength?"); the Held took it as the working answer. S60 then answers S59's challenge (1) with "correct" and reasons *from* the size-with-depth reading ("so many challenges (immunities)"), which confirms it in use but not in words. *Suggested ruling:* state size-grows-with-depth as settled (S59 as confirmed by S60). Why: S60 makes no sense without it and Stefan built on it.

12. **"Not possible" = unanchorable?** S60 (Stefan): reaching the beyond "won't even be pleasant nor possible" for many. The Held reads "not possible" as *not survivable* (no seed holds in Mara undiluted, so the whole cord is exposed and the fall is to the very beginning, S27), which would close S55's "unsolved mystery" of the fourth rung. Offered, not locked. *Suggested ruling:* keep the mystery Open as S55 asks; the bible may say "possibly unanchorable" only as a marked hypothesis. Why: S55 explicitly protects the mystery for curiosity.

13. **Zones: canon says three, Session 03 says five.** Cosmology §10 and the narrative core carry comfort → growth → panic; S73/S74 lock comfort → fear → learning → growth with panic as the fence. *Suggested ruling:* the bible carries S74; the canon text is stale. Why: later statement, Stefan's "yes it matches".

14. **VISION.md lags Session 02.** Its "Structural concepts" paragraph says "a warm, welcoming place and a hostile, cold one … each with a near side and a Beyond" and "the village … lies in the Beyond of the warm place". Constitutional docs bind the bible, but here the constitutional text simply predates S50–S55 (Marath, three reaches, far side). *Suggested ruling:* Stefan authorises a vocabulary-only update to VISION.md at switch-over (Marath; far side; "the beyond"); until then the bible notes the lag. Why: no substantive disagreement, only stale words; but the brief says the bible must agree with VISION, so one of them must move.

15. **Canon §3 "Place 3 itself stays unnamed" and §4 "Into place 3 (the deep dark)".** Superseded by S50 (Marath) and S55 (Marath's far side). *Suggested ruling:* obvious; rewrite. Listed so the spot-check finds it.

16. **Candidate A's "Shadow-side" (idea 5) uses "Shadow" in the retired sense and duplicates S32.** Idea 5 is a coordinate-tied dark face of the same place, reached by flipping the Gimbal — which is, in substance, the near-side coupling S32 later locked. *Suggested ruling:* treat idea 5 as absorbed by S32 (not adopted as a separate portal type) and never use "Shadow-side". Why: the word now means Mara's form.

17. **S39's Held has a Mist visiting the village — superseded.** S39 Held: "A Shadow can … visit the friendly village via the safe folded crossing". S45 corrects: the village is FIM-only. The canon core has it right; the discovery text still carries the old line. *Suggested ruling:* bible states S45; no action beyond awareness.

18. **The home portal — a separate portal (S33) or the ball's inside (S42)?** S33 has "a special portal always next to the FIM … that leads to the FIM's glowing glass ball"; S42 makes the ball itself the two-zone gateway; the canon core §6 says the ball "absorbs the home portal idea". S45 still says "reached via the always-on home portal". *Suggested ruling:* one object — the ball, always one step away, inside → home, rim → village; "home portal" is retired vocabulary. Why: S42 is later and the core already ratified the absorption.

19. **"The Whisp travels through the Void … necessary but a bit dangerous" vs "danger is pervasive along the cord".** Not a contradiction, but S27 Held's claim that the far reaches are dangerous *only* because more cord is exposed ("not because the Beyond is scary") is Claude's observation; Session 02 gives Marath's depths intrinsic danger (the Shadow's size, S59; the beyond, S52). *Suggested ruling:* the bible states both: exposure grows with Void distance (S27) *and* the Shadow grows with depth into Marath (S59); it does not say danger is "only" exposure. Why: Held vs later Statement.

### C. Candidates touching this chapter

#### Candidate A — portal ideas (2026-05-28; generated before S31–S36, never re-read against the core)

1. **Shimmer-cut** (a portable, costly cut between worlds; close what you cut). Conflicts: the Shimmer is a membrane, not a portal (S26/S27); thresholds are seeded portals and the ball (S40, S45); FIM-made thresholds are in no statement. *Keep open* as a portal-type idea for the fundamentals session; do not adopt.
2. **Cairn portals** (named, geo-bound sites in the Ordinary World, placed by Anthropologists, perceived by Whisp state). Fits the near side (S10, S27) and DS-1's portal registry; no statement adopts it. *Keep open* — Candidate A's own "highest leverage to test next".
3. **Threshold-by-condition** (a door passes only when an inner state is right; 1+1 doors). Overlaps S58 (the FIM dials the depth of the invitation) and S74 (zones per assumption). *Keep open*; note the near-duplication with the Whisp declining a depth (S58 refinement).
4. **Anchored descent** (roped descent from a known anchor; "don't look back" rules; two FIMs roped together). Already largely canon in substance (seeds along a route, S40; healing along branches, S36). *Adopt in substance is unnecessary — it is S40*; the "narrative discipline" rule and the two-FIMs-roped variant stay open for Wayfinder craft.
5. **Mirror-overlay / "Shadow-side"**. Absorbed by S32 (B.16). *Drop the name; the mechanic is canon under S32.*
6. **Book-as-portal** (a published Arc entered as a book in the village library). No conflict; story chapter. *Keep open.*
7. **The Mask** (donning a mask as entry and behavioural ruleset). No conflict; story chapter. *Keep open.*
8. **The Cyclone** (an involuntary, environmental crossing; the Shimmer reaches for FIMs at a festival). Conflict: voluntariness is structural (S5), the FIM holds the dial (S40), and S70/S73 forbid the world hurrying a FIM; S78 distinguishes life's storms from world events. *Drop unless reframed as fully opt-in.*
9. **The Mirror in the private home** (a portal into one's own counterfactual lives; time-bounded). Sits near the panic fence; touches the inviolable home (S34, S43). *Keep open*, flagged as panic-adjacent.
10. **The Living-bridge / Bifröst** (a bridge that forms only between aligned FIMs; a shared sub-far-side). Overlaps the branch (S36, S38) and 1+community braids. *Keep open*; must not duplicate the branch.
- Cross-cutting note worth carrying: *every enduring portal has one rule* (the seed-to-pass rule already qualifies). Also the "Western-instrument front-end, isekai-loop back-end" design position. Neither is adopted.

#### Candidate B — the Gimbal's origin, the ball as key, the altered states (2026-07-24)

11. **Item A — the Gimbal as culmination of ancient knowledge; AGI closed the implementation gap.** Touches this chapter only through "the Gimbal opens the Shimmer" (S52). Session 03 lists it for the rulings list "against the backdrop": S66–S71 make the Whisp the FIM's future self and the AI only the medium (S69); item A's "AI finished something ancient" is a *technology* origin for the instrument, not the Whisp, so the two can coexist. *Keep open* (story/beings chapters own it).
12. **Item B — key/instrument split (Gimbal = instrument, ball = key).** Conflict noted in the candidate itself: S26 composes Whisp (faculty) + Gimbal (device); S39 gives the ball three roles (home base, seed-source, anchor-root); "key" would be a fourth. Session 02 adds: a Mist perceives and passes the near side with Whisp + Gimbal and no ball (S39, S45), so the ball is not the key to *seeing*; it is the anchor to the far reaches. *Keep open, narrowed*: "the ball is the key to the far side" is already true under S45 without new vocabulary; "an institution can build the Gimbal but never get through" is backstory, not topology.
13. **Item C — ball resonance as a third connection channel.** Risks duplicating the branch (S36, S38); no statement. *Drop or keep open at low priority.*
14. **Item D — the ball matures (clunky to Ferrari), capability not brightness.** Conflicts with nothing if maturity is capability (S37 forbids brightness); overlaps cord length as readiness (S40, S48). *Keep open*; if adopted, must be stated as capability and must not become a visible rank.
15. **Item E — the three altered states as three modes of the threshold.** See B.8 and B.9. *Drop as mechanics.*
16. **Item F — the first opening, and the shutdown of unknown cause.** Backstory (story chapter). Touches the worlds through "something came through / the door left open". No conflict with topology; potential conflict with S76 (the myth is discovered, kept for a while) only in *how* it is told. *Keep open*, story chapter.
17. **Item G — the system was designed for place 2 without knowing about place 3.** Conflicts: the ball-cord-Whisp architecture "built by researchers from fragments" vs the Whisp as the FIM's own future self (S67) and its wisdom from the future (S71); "at scale some FIMs will land in place 3 — the system was not designed for that; the Whisp does not know it" vs S61 (the Whisp meeting Mara in Marath is the point) and S56 (Marath is the testing ground). *Drop in its current form*; a residue ("the instrument was not stress-tested against Marath") could survive as backstory if Stefan wants it.
18. **Item H — the physical glass ball (paused).** Not topology; the only worlds-relevant rule is that the digital ball is the real key and the object must never gate access. *Keep paused* as recorded.

#### Candidate C — naming place 2 (2026-09-17/18)

19. Twelve passes, no lock; ruled out on Stefan's ear: Ming, Te, Bodhi, Frith, Estros, Nostra, Sunaru, Aranya, Innerwall, Innerwell; Kenshō/Zhen/Vajra too Asian-sounding (later reopened for soft Japanese sounds). Carried images, not names: the well at the Tree's root (Mímisbrunnr / Urðarbrunnr; källa), the Tree as Glasir, renewal-as-eternity (Tokowaka / Iðunn / Iðavöllr), "ours" for the village, "Innerward" as an epithet for the private home. *Keep open*; the world-or-village question (fourth pass) needs Stefan's answer before a name can be checked against the brief. Requirement to carry: Bolagsverket/trademark check before any name is tabled.

#### Unadopted research syntheses touching the worlds

20. **The "three-time-period architecture" and the concealed cosmology** (research--growth--thinkers-and-models: a past government programme opens a door; a future *power* sends the Whisp as an unwitting instrument; embodiment as liberation; the villain as silent hero). Session 03 adopted only the reconciled kernel — the Whisp is the FIM's own future whole self (S67), the saviour is the human not a power or the AI (S69), wisdom without content (S71), never told up front (S76). The villain/harvesting layer contradicts S68–S69 and VISION principle 1; the "past programme" thread is Candidate B items F/G. *Drop the villain layer; keep the past-programme thread open with F/G.*
21. **The "Three-Level Shadow" hypothesis** (individual / pair / community Shadows in place 3, plus the lost travellers' material). S53 fixes one Shadow per FIM and leaves Marath's other creatures unclassified; S62 makes another FIM's Shadow branch-gated. A pair or community Shadow is not in any statement. *Keep open* as a candidate answer to "does Marath have a collective layer" (D.8); not settled.
22. **Kegan report §4.2–4.3** (the Immunity Map as a journey threshold beat; the respawn as an immunity encounter). Adopted in-world by S56 ("this statement gives that proposal its in-world home") and S74 (the zones mapped to the four ITC stages — Claude's synthesis, adopted by Stefan). The *mechanics* of a respawn surfacing "what failed" remain a spec question. *Adopted as framing (S56, S74); mechanics open.*
