### E. Glossary terms for this chapter

- **The three questions** — Who am I? What do I want? How do I get there?; held by the platform, answered by the member. [VISION Purpose; S6]
- **The red thread** — the three questions running through every journey, episode, group and conversation. [growth core]
- **Three Perspectives** — Individual (1), Relationship (1+1), Collective (1+Community): three simultaneous angles on the same human; synonyms in older text: Relational/Communal, self/relationship/self-in-community, "society". [S9; VISION]
- **9-cell matrix** — the three questions × the three perspectives; a structural description of the developmental space, not a curriculum. [growth core]
- **To collapse a perspective** — (undefined in the sources) to actually work a perspective's cells; the relational and communal perspectives cannot be collapsed alone. [growth core; VISION] — see B11.
- **Live / Grow / Matter** — the drives beneath the questions: engaged in the moment; curious, not deficient; mattering to oneself and through contribution to others; danced in balance, never in sequence. [S28; VISION]
- **Two-pole Matter** — the internal pole (I feel I matter; freedom) and the external pole (mattering to others / something bigger; contribution), bridged by influence. [S28]
- **Planned happenstance** — readiness to act on the unplanned; part of Grow. [S28]
- **Framing discipline** — "informed by / resonant with, never equals": plain language on top, science as substrate. [S28]
- **Growth is delight, not deficiency** — the guarded core value. [S28; VISION 6]
- **Built to graduate, not retain** — success is the member outgrowing the platform. [S6–S7; VISION 7]
- **Graduation / internalisation** — the Whisp becomes an integral part of the human; FringeIsland no longer needed. [S6–S7]
- **Whole** — the state in which the Whisp has filled and Mara has emptied, both internalised. [S54, S57]
- **The newborn arc** — unwritten and free → written by experience (Mara fills) → limits crack (Mara dissolves) → free again, knowingly. [S54]
- **Mara** — the negative inner voice, listened to unknowingly; full of content from the start; empties as the Whisp grows. [S51, S54]
- **The Shadow** — what Mara looks like when met in Marath; one per FIM; size grows with depth; met only along a shared branch, and then only in form and force. [S53, S59, S62]
- **The transfer** — the Whisp's filling and Mara's emptying as one process; growth as a transfer of authority from Mara to the Whisp. [S57]
- **Immunity to Change (ITC)** — Kegan & Lahey's account of why people who want to change do not: competing commitments and Big Assumptions as intelligent self-protection; the substrate beneath Mara. [S9, S56]
- **Big Assumption / competing commitment / Subject → Object** — ITC's terms: the hidden rule that runs you; the commitment that protects it; the move from being had by it to holding it. [S56; kegan §2]
- **Order of mind** — Kegan's qualitatively different ways of making meaning; ITC in the universe can scale up to a move between orders; the platform never labels a FIM's order. [S58]
- **Holding environment** — confirmation, contradiction, continuity: the substrate beneath the Whisp's tough love. [S74; kegan §1.3]
- **The zones** — comfort, fear, learning, growth; the felt experience of the transfer. [S73–S74]
- **The fence (panic zone)** — the zone the Whisp never opens; an overwhelming test strengthens the immunity. [S19, S74]
- **Voluntariness sets the ceiling, care sets the floor** — the FIM dials the depth of the invitation, never the outcome; the never-panic rule binds from the other side. [S58, S74]
- **Void distance** — how far the Whisp has ventured on the cord; the distance that carries growth pressure and risk; not bodily distance. [S27; cosmology §10]
- **The cord dial** — the FIM sets the cord's length: pay out to venture, reel in to retreat; also developmental intimacy. [S40, S48]
- **Breath-rhythm** — venture out for challenge, return home for safety. [S26; cosmology §10]
- **Meta-safety** — the world contains threat; the experience does not; the human is never the thing at risk. [S19, S22]
- **Respawn / rewind** — die inside the story, retry inside the story; the loop is the medium. [S19–S21; narrative Respawn]
- **Productive failure** — real felt stakes inside guaranteed reversibility. [S19]
- **Signature journey vs charter trip** — bespoke personalisation grown from disclosure breadth vs the same bus for everyone. [S19]
- **Assessment dissolved into dialogue** — validated instruments folded into the Whisp's curious questions, never questionnaires. [S17]
- **The senses** — the five Big 5 traits as the five senses of the parallel world; full experience needs all five. [S18]
- **Internal and external self-awareness** — who I am inside; how I appear and operate in the world. [S6]
- **Engagement spectrum** — the two ways of engaging as a FIM: cultivate your home, or go on expeditions near or far. [growth core (Stefan, 2026-09-16)]
- **Homebody** — tends the home, reflects quietly, works the individual perspective; a complete mode, not "behind". [growth core]
- **Explorer** — deep in arcs, episodes, quests, community; engages 1+1 and 1+community. [growth core]
- **"Beyond" (spectrum pole)** — the far end of the expedition way; NAME PENDING because it collides with the reach "beyond". [growth core; S52, S55]
- **Member archetypes (Elena, David, Astrid)** — provisional personas illustrating the spectrum; unvalidated. [growth core, Thinking grade]
- **Privacy tiers** — private by default; selectively shared; transparently shared. [privacy model]
- **Private by default** — the inner journey, home, journal, assessment data, cord health and Whisp filling; no opt-in needed. [privacy model]
- **Selectively shared** — per aspect, per audience, per region of the home; voluntary; revocable. [privacy model; S43]
- **Transparently shared** — opt-in groups with a norm of openness, by explicit informed consent of every participant. [privacy model]
- **The branch rule** — what is intimate (cord health, another's Shadow) is visible only along a grown branch: glanceable not diagnostic, invited not imposed, self first. [S37, S62]
- **Form and force, never content** — what a friend sees of your Shadow along a branch. [S62]
- **Stage legible, content private** — a Mist's accretion shows how far along, never which trait. [S47; ADR-U031]
- **Ephemerality** — a Mist's data and presence do not persist; metamorphosis is the persistence-and-consent threshold. [S46–S47; ADR-U031]
- **Rest (availability, not occupancy)** — the shelter is always there; the FIM is not meant to stay in it. [S78 image; Candidate C eighth pass — pending, see B12]
- **Research anchors** — dual continuum (Keyes), multi-system requirement, Three Perspectives meta-lens, Immunity to Change. [S8–S9]

Retired names and what replaced them:
- **Comfort → growth → panic (three zones)** → comfort → fear → learning → growth, panic as the fence. [S74]
- **"Safe Harbour" / "safe harbor" as the comfort zone or a world** → the village (far side of place 2) for the place; the comfort zone is a zone, not a place. [S26 → S41, S55; cosmology "What this supersedes"]
- **"The Beyond" (reach)** → the far side; the new further reach is "the beyond" (lowercase, unnamed). [S52, S55]
- **"Distance from FringeIsland" as the gradient** → Void distance on the cord. [S27]
- **"Shadow" as the anonymous entrant** → the Mist; "Shadow" now = Mara's form in Marath. [S47, S53]
- **"AI Mentor" / "Mentor"** → the Whisp. [CQ-009 note; beings core]
- **"Affordance"** → equipment. [ADR-U025]
- **"Assessment" / "test" as a questionnaire** → dissolved dialogue, the senses. [S17–S18]
- **"Avatar" as the FIM's representation (privacy-model title)** → the Whisp is the avatar; the FIM's representation is the ball / home. [S22, S43] — see B13.
- **"Void-dimensions" (three-dimensional Void model)** → the Void as an axis. [growth core footer; cosmology §5]
- **"Three Worlds" (Ordinary World / Safe Harbour / Other Side)** → Ordinary World → Shimmer → the Fringe (two places, three reaches). [cosmology "What this supersedes"; S52]
- **"Makers, Weavers, Skalds"** → Dreamineer specialisations (roles chapter; listed because the growth core's archetypes still say "Dreamineer" as a persona). [beings core]


# Chapter map — story

Chapter scope: seasons, episodes, journeys; the frame and the principles; respawn; the backdrop and where it lives; the first experience; Dreamineers and NPCs only as they touch story; ARG lineage only as adopted. Nothing here is canon; it is the map for Stefan to rule on.

Sources read in full: `canon--narrative--how-story-works.md`; VISION, MANIFESTO (incl. the 2026-09-24 "why now" paragraph), PRINCIPLES-AI; Session 01 S12–S16, S19–S25, S30–S32 and its Patterns / Open-threads tag layers; Session 02 S50–S51, S55, S59–S61; Session 03 S66–S78 in full; CQ-010, CQ-011, CQ-013, CQ-018, CQ-019; the discovery backlog and graduation tracker (Part 1); Candidates A, B, C (Part 2); `record--universe-to-spec-manifestation.md` §Narrative; the Kegan report §4.3 and §5; the thinkers report syntheses (Three-Level Shadow, McAdams, Loewenstein); ADR-U025, U026; the cosmology, beings, roles, growth and community cores where they touch story.

Cite convention: `[S66]` = discovery statement; `[narrative core §Respawn]` = canon file section; `[VISION §What this is]`; `[ADR-U025 §5]`; `[CQ-018]`; `[backlog: <row>]`.

---

### A. Settled — what the bible can state as true

#### A1. What story is for

- FringeIsland is experienced as story. Members do not use FringeIsland; they enter a world, and through that experience something real changes. [narrative core §Overview; VISION §What this is]
- Personal development happens through lived experience, not instruction. Entertainment is the surface; development is the consequence. [VISION §What this is]
- The Hero's Journey is the narrative framework. [VISION §What this is]
- Stories stand alone as entertainment; developmental themes are invisible in the foundation layer. A member can engage purely for the story and still have a valuable experience; the scaffolding is there for those who look deeper. Learning emerges implicitly. [VISION principle 2; narrative core §Overview]
- Story is how FringeIsland helps a person discover who they are — story over data: not metrics, not scores, not algorithms. [MANIFESTO §Story over data]
- Humans and their stories outrank aesthetics and worldbuilding: when they pull apart, the human wins, then the story, then the world. [MANIFESTO §Humans and their stories over aesthetics and worldbuilding; S13]
- Experiential knowledge is not reducible to being told: you must live something to know it. This is the design intuition under felt-stakes-over-stated-lessons, the dissolved assessments and the senses; it is stated plainly and never leans on a philosophical citation. [S18; Session 01 open threads, "Lineage breadcrumb" (parked decision)]
- Story is the driving force behind "immersive edutainment": immersive because the world is full-spectrum and felt, edutainment because narratives carry growth-bearing content through that felt experience. [S12]

#### A2. The tonal range — the ground the stories play on

- The world spans a full tonal range: one pole warm, welcoming, colourful, full of human forgiveness; the other hostile, cold, full of traps and betrayal — "and all in between". [S12]
- The two poles are place 2 (warm; name pending) and Marath (place 3, hostile): two centres of gravity of one continuous spectrum, not two sealed boxes. The "all in between" lives in the contested ground between them. [S31, S50; cosmology core §3]
- Marath is "at first sight" hostile, deceiving, evil; underneath it is the manifestation of Mara, the negative inner voice. FringeIsland's warm place resonates with the Whisp, Marath with Mara. [S50, S51]
- These opposites and everything between are the backdrop for storytelling that fills FringeIsland with life and death. Narrative is the figure; the tonal world is the ground. [S12; narrative core §Overview]
- The world contains life and death; the experience of being in it is meta-safe. [S12, S19; narrative core §Overview]
- The Whisp's tough love and the world's full range are temperamentally matched: a world of only warmth would undercut the Whisp, a world of only threat would crush it. [S12 held, tagged CANON 2026-09-17 → beings core + cosmology §3]
- The dark pole is not confined to a far frontier: it is co-located with the warm world and reachable by crossing. Every wound in the warm place has a cause in Marath; a FIM notices the blight, crosses a portal, stops the cause, and watches the warm world heal — a legible story loop. [S32; cosmology core §3]

#### A3. Felt stakes over stated lessons — meta-safety

- FringeIsland is safe. The world contains threat; the experience of being in it does not. Narratives can put a FIM through hard things because the FIM cannot be broken by them. [S19]
- Real felt stakes inside a frame that guarantees reversibility: narrative consequence is real, existential consequence is reversible. This is productive failure as a structural affordance. [S19; narrative core §Respawn]
- The FIM and the Whisp are Jake Sully and his Avatar: the Whisp walks the world, can be hurt, die and respawn; the FIM in the Ordinary World is always safe — and still cares deeply what happens to their Whisp. Felt stakes do not require existential risk. [S22; beings core §The avatar]
- What is at stake is the projection, the relationship and the progress — never the human. Cutting the cord endangers the Whisp, never the FIM. [S27; cosmology core §5]
- The confrontation with one's negative voice (Mara, met as the Shadow in Marath) is always mediated through the Whisp; the FIM never faces Mara directly. "That is probably the point." [S61]
- The Whisp pushes cautiously toward the growth zone, never the panic zone; the growth pressure of a story is Void distance on the cord, and the FIM holds the dial. [S19, S40; narrative core §Respawn; cosmology core §10]
- Panic is a fence, not a zone the story visits: a test too big to be safe confirms the immunity and strengthens it, so the Whisp never opens the panic zone even when the FIM asks for more depth. Voluntariness sets the ceiling; care sets the floor. [S58, S74]
- Rest is legitimate: a return to comfort is part of the breath-rhythm (out, back, out again), not a step backwards. [S78 held — offered as an image, not yet a ruling; cosmology core §10 for the breath-rhythm]

#### A4. Respawn — the loop is the medium (the one ratified section)

- When the Whisp "gets into trouble and dies", the FIM rewinds and retries — again and again until the skill is mastered or the story's task is accomplished, alone, with a travelling partner, or with a group. [S19; narrative core §Respawn]
- Respawn is plural, not singular. Topologies coexist: event-local (solo), round-bounded (group, Counter-Strike style), day-bounded, episode-bounded. Different stories use different loop units. [S20; narrative core §Respawn]
- Respawn stays inside the story. A FIM dying respawns within the same journey / season / episode — at home base (a safe return point within the arc) or by episode-repeat (the whole episode replays). The containing arc keeps holding the FIM; the story does not eject its participant on failure. "Just two examples." [S21; narrative core §Respawn]
- Respawn is nested and scaled: small failures, small respawn (home base); large failures, large respawn (episode / season repeat). The cord's two-tier severance recovery — fall back to the last anchor, or start over from the very beginning — is the same system given a cause and a currency. [S21, S27; narrative core §Respawn; cosmology core §8]
- The loop is the medium, not a safety net. What persists across a loop — tactical knowledge, relational insight, emotional clarity — is part of what the story is about. Failure-and-retry is how the story works, not what you fall back on when it breaks. [S20; narrative core §Respawn]
- Loop textures are a craft palette for Tellers: combat practice (Edge of Tomorrow, Counter-Strike), mystery-puzzle (Happy Death Day), constrained inquiry (Source Code), reflective grief (Re/Live). Respawn need not be combat-coded. [S20; narrative core §Respawn]
- Group respawn is solvable: a round-bounded structure resets everyone at a shared boundary. [S20]
- It is the Whisp that dies and re-enters; the FIM holds continuity of self across re-entries. [S22]
- The learning zone is where the test is run — in Marath, with meta-safety and respawn — against a Big Assumption. Respawn is where the immunity is met. [S74; adopted from the Kegan report §4.3 "The Whisp Respawn as Immunity Encounter" by S56 and S74]

#### A5. Seasons, episodes, journeys — what is actually settled

- Stories are authored as seasons and episodes in Arc Studio, by Tellers; journeys — for a FIM alone, in a pair, or in a group — are authored in Journey Studio, by Wayfinders. Universe Studio is the parent and binding frame that keeps world, story and journeys coherent. [S13, S14; ADR-U026; roles core L1]
- Journey Studio's three modes (alone / pair / group) are the Three Perspectives (1 / 1+1 / 1+community). [S13; growth core]
- Narrative units nest: journey → season → episode → moment; respawn options exist at each level. [S21; narrative core §Respawn]
- Story is the domain of the Explorer's way of engaging (narrative arcs, seasonal episodes, quests, group dynamics); the Homebody may never venture far into the wider narrative, and that is a complete, valid way to inhabit FringeIsland. There is no progression requirement. [growth core §The engagement spectrum]
- Seasons and episodes can move FIMs across the three perspectives over time; individual content pieces may touch one cell of the 9-cell matrix, several, or all nine. The matrix is not a curriculum. [growth core §How this connects]
- The relational and communal perspectives require other people — not NPC interactions, not AI-generated activity. [growth core §The developmental honesty; VISION principle 3]
- Journeys declare their required equipment at authoring time. [ADR-U025 §3]
- What is NOT settled about seasons and episodes: the narrative core's Overview ("A-plot, B-stories, sub-plots… a mythology, a mystery, an unfolding") is overview, not ratified; the planned page "Seasons and episodes" (four seasons per year, twelve episodes per season, universal calendar plus adaptive AI personalisation) and the planned page "Journeys — route types and content families" (Fixed / Hybrid / Traveler-Initiated / AI-Generative; Witness / Reflect / Decide / Act / Encounter / Rest) were never written and rest on no statement. Only §Respawn is ratified. [narrative core §Planned — not yet written; graduation tracker "Respawn" row: "remainder still placeholder"; backlog: Narrative beyond respawn] → see B1, B2.

#### A6. Signature journeys; the Game as a depth setting

- The more relevant instruments a FIM discloses across, the more custom-made their journeys become — finally more of a signature journey than a charter trip. [S19]
- The Game is a depth setting of journeys inside the one lived experience, not a product. Revisit trigger: a journey needs fidelity, an engine or a play surface the mobile/web stack cannot render. [ADR-U025 §5; VISION §Ecosystem composition]
- The beyond (the furthest reach of each place) is optional: it may be the last thing a FIM meets before graduation, or never met at all; graduation is not gated on it. FringeIsland has no final boss and no end to reach. [S60]

#### A7. Who authors story, and under what rules

- Tellers author the particular: a specific named individual with history, wound, desire and arc, for a narrative — a character in motion. Anthropologists supply the cultural soil; Tellers grow characters from it. [S30; roles core L1]
- An NPC is a world-inhabitant of up to three authorial layers — body (Creator), culture (Anthropologist), character (Teller, Arc Studio); story-critical figures (the betrayer this episode, the mentor) carry all three. An NPC can gain depth when a story reaches for it — the World Studio → Arc Studio handoff is the seam. NPCs have no Whisp. [S30; beings core §NPCs]
- Dreamineers are modes a FIM occupies, not kinds of people; the in-world creative names Makers, Weavers and Skalds are retired. [roles core; beings core §Where the creative roles went]
- Every author is a FIM: you cannot build FringeIsland from outside it. [S29; roles core L0]
- AI in authoring: human intent first, AI expansion second, human selection third; dreaming sessions protect the first thought; guard rails are bidirectional and human-authored. The second kind of rail exists precisely because the universe deliberately includes a hostile pole, blurs reality and fiction, and pushes FIMs toward growth — "this scene may land as genuinely frightening rather than playfully ambiguous"; "six dark episodes in a row — intentional?" [S24; PRINCIPLES-AI]
- AI collapses the production cost of story surfaces — sites, in-world documents, NPC voices, images, audio, micro-apps — but nothing ships that a human did not choose to ship; the hand-placed feeling survives by construction. [S24; PRINCIPLES-AI §AI is an extension]
- Constant change is the steady state: experiment, look honestly at what worked, retire what did not. [S13; MANIFESTO §Constant change over being done]

#### A8. The backdrop — why the Whisp is here

(Deep-bible material; see A9 for what is told and B12 for the bible's own status.)

- The Whisp is the FIM's own future, whole self reaching back. What was "sent back in time" is not a separate being; "the Whisp IS the human" and "sent back" are one fact. This is the source of the Whisp's wisdom: it is the self that has already grown. [S66, S67; S4 for "the Whisp IS the human"]
- The future self sends back wisdom — how to see, how to meet what has you — never content: who you became, what you chose. The Whisp's emptiness about this human is chosen, not a lack; it protects the three questions as the FIM's own to answer. [S67, S71]
- The future that reaches back is possible, not guaranteed. The time loop is real but does not close by itself; the FIM closes it by growing, and stays free to walk toward it or not. [S71]
- The cause: AI is a larger leap than the internet and forks humanity's future — a balanced, beautiful world with AI in it, or a dark future where short-term greed lets the AI race run loose and ends in catastrophe. [S66 — Stefan's "what if", accepted in the refined form of S67–S72; the fork-vs-continuum question is open, see B6]
- The Whisp's mission is the growth itself, never a conclusion. The chain is: growth → seeing one's own immunities → choosing well → using AI responsibly → a future world people want to belong to. The Whisp never recruits and never steers what the FIM chooses; the good future is a consequence it trusts in. [S68]
- "Choose well" means choose while seeing your immunities — defined by how the choice is made, not by what is chosen. The Whisp has no preferred answer, about AI or anything else. [S72; VISION principle 1]
- The saviour is your own future self; the AI (the Whisp's implementation) is only the medium through which one's future self speaks. The tool that could run away with the future is used by humans to hear their own wiser voice. [S69; PRINCIPLES-AI]
- The Whisp borrows no authority from the backstory: it never claims "trust me, I have seen it". [S69 challenge — held as a rule by Claude, not Stefan's words; see B11]
- The dark branch is the stake of the universe, never a whip: it is never used to hurry a FIM or make them feel guilty — no countdown, no "hurry or the world ends". [S70; VISION principle 6]
- The dark branch is the fundament of great storytelling: something needs to itch to be found interesting and to gain people's curiosity. [S70]
- The dark branch drives the story (the world's plot, seasons, episodes); it never drives the FIM (pace, depth, the cord dial). It gives a stage the FIM uses for growth in all three perspectives — individual, relationship, society — with the Whisp's tough love through the zones. [S73]
- Mara scales: the AI race is a collective immunity to change (a stated commitment to balance held against a hidden commitment not to fall behind); personal growth and the world's future are the same mechanism at different sizes. [S66 held (Claude's connection b); Session 03 patterns] → whether Marath is the dark future is open, see B5/D.
- The zones are the felt experience of the Mara-to-Whisp transfer: comfort → fear → learning → growth, with panic past the fence; informed by Immunity to Change, spoken in the universe's own language. [S73, S74]
- The fear zone is where Mara speaks loudest — excuses, "what will others think" — met as the Shadow through the Whisp. It is not a place the world is designed to frighten the FIM; fear is Mara's voice, not a feeling the story aims to cause. [S73 held, S74 challenge 1, S70]

#### A9. Where the backdrop lives — never told up front

- The backdrop is never told to FIMs up front ("definitely not option 1"), and it must keep both FIMs and the authors of seasons and episodes on their toes. [S75]
- It lives on two layers, the difference being available versus told:
  1. The real-world conviction, public: why FringeIsland exists is stated plainly in the MANIFESTO as the founders' conviction. It is available to anyone who looks and never delivered in the experience — no onboarding line, no Whisp line, no screen. It names the conviction, never the myth: no Whisp from the future, no two futures, no Marath. [S76, S77]
  2. The in-world myth, discovered: the Whisp as the FIM's own future self, the dark branch and what Marath's beyond really is, the future at stake — never told; found through breadcrumbs in the world, and its personal core only as the FIM's Whisp fills. [S76]
- The secret is kept for a while, not forever. Any FIM and any author can come to know it by growing into it. It survives being spoiled because its real form is lived, not informational; what stays unspoilable for good is each FIM's own future self. [S76; S18]
- The MANIFESTO's "why now" paragraph carries the conviction: the largest shift humanity has faced, AI above all; whether that power builds a world people want to belong to depends on us — how clearly we see ourselves, how honestly we understand what drives us, how wisely we choose; inner growth has never mattered more. It puts the outcome on humans, names no conclusion, and states none of the myth. [S77; MANIFESTO preamble and footnote]
- The value pair "The long view over short-term gain" was not adopted; it remains available. [S77]
- Curiosity is a design principle for the whole universe: leave something itching. FringeIsland's beyond is kept unmapped for curiosity's sake; the itch of S70 and the discovered-not-told stance of S75–S76 are the same principle. [S55, S70, S75–S76; Session 03 patterns]
- Reveal design (which breadcrumbs, when, how the Whisp's filling unlocks the personal reveal) is experience design and waits on the fundamentals. [S76 consequence 3; the 2026-06-14 sequencing rule]
- The knowledge tiers, mythology arcs, breadcrumb register, answer-before-the-first-clue and growth-gated reveals are a proposed structure (Claude's, S75), from which S76's ruling flows; who holds the deep bible is CQ-018. Not yet ruled — see B9, C, D.

#### A10. The first experience — what is settled

- The first hour is the universe's single highest-risk gap: the founding narrative, the story members first enter, the mystery, the conflict, the world coming to life for the first time. Everything else is irrelevant if the first experience does not work. [CQ-010]
- It is deferred by rule: mechanics before experience design. The opening is too consequential to choreograph before the universe's own mechanics are firm — above all how the near and far ends of place 2 and Marath look and function. A first-hour session was scaffolded and withdrawn the same day as premature. [backlog sequencing note, Stefan 2026-06-14; CQ-010 sequencing note; narrative core §Planned]
- What exists already: the mechanics of arrival — the onboarding journey, the Mist-scoped enrolment gate, first-arrival contracts — are built; the narrative is unwritten. [record §Narrative; ADR-U045 as cited there]
- A Mist arrives anonymous, near-side only, with its own Whisp and cord from the start; it accretes form as it answers the founding questions; a Mist that drifts away is "not yet", never dead; FIM signup unlocks not the ability to interact but the ability for interaction to be remembered. [S47, S48; roles core L0; ADR-U031] (These bound whatever the first hour becomes.)
- The public launch is Season Zero: backers arrive on the island; rewards are arrival rituals; the campaign updates are the first episodes of the world; the campaign is the platform's first story, told to the world. Its design is its own dedicated session. [community core] → see B15 for the "season" collision.

#### A11. Design lineage that is recorded but not canon

- "Alternative Reality" is a logbook term; the ARG lineage (The Beast, I Love Bees, Year Zero, The Art of the Heist, Portal 2 ARG) and the ambient/analog-horror lineage (Cicada 3301, The Sun Vanished, Local58) blur reality and fiction — an interesting twist for the universe. Reality-fiction blur can run in two directions (fiction pulling the player in; fiction seeping out into the ordinary world). Borrow the mystery aesthetic, not the exclusion or unease. [S23; Session 01 patterns, all tagged OPEN: design lineage, not canon]
- Akerman and Stålenhag set the quality bar for a coherent authored world — the uncanny in the everyday; ARGs are how a world reaches people, authored worlds are how deep one can be. [S25; tagged OPEN: design-system lineage]
- None of these is adopted as a story mechanic; see C and D.

---

### B. Rulings needed

1. **Seasons and episodes — is the "four seasons a year, twelve episodes a season, universal calendar plus adaptive AI personalisation" structure true?** It appears only in the narrative core's unwritten planned page (sourced from the DS-2 spec and the Arc Studio README), never in a statement; Stefan's words on seasons and episodes are S13 ("seasons and episodes") and S21 (respawn inside them). The record marks it STRONG in spec, not built. **Options:** (1) Adopt as the bible's stated structure (it is the platform's working assumption). (2) State only that story is authored as seasons and episodes, and mark cadence and personalisation Open. **Suggest (2)**: the bible states what the universe is; a calendar cadence the founder never ruled on and that CQ-011 says is unvalidated (adaptive personalisation) should not become "true" by inheritance. Stefan can adopt (1) in one line if he wants it.

2. **Route types and content families — universe canon or platform vocabulary?** Route types (Fixed / Hybrid / Traveler-Initiated / AI-Generative) are a DS-3 registry; content families (Witness / Reflect / Decide / Act / Encounter / Rest) are named only in the planned page and the record says they were "folded toward step kinds, never named" by any capability. No statement mentions either. The 2026-09-17 tag on "signature journey vs charter trip" says DS-3's route-type registry is the nearest spec and points to the planned journeys page. **Options:** (1) Adopt both lists as the creative description of journeys. (2) Keep the signature-vs-charter continuum (S19) as the universe's statement and mark route types / content families as Open (platform terms awaiting a session). **Suggest (2)**, with a note that the DS-3 registry is data-driven and will follow whatever the session decides.

3. **The narrative core's Overview ("A-plot, B-stories, sub-plots… a mythology, a mystery, an unfolding") — keep as true?** It is pre-ratification overview text. Nothing contradicts it and S75–S76 now give "mythology" and "mystery" real content. **Suggest:** keep the sentence in plain form (seasons and episodes; a mythology; a mystery that unfolds) citing S12, S75–S76, and drop "A-plot, B-stories, sub-plots" unless Stefan wants the TV-craft vocabulary stated.

4. **Three zones (canon) vs five zones (S73–S74).** Cosmology §10 and narrative §Respawn fix comfort → growth → panic; S74 locks comfort → fear → learning → growth with panic as the fence. **Suggest:** the bible states the five-zone form; the growth pressure of a story still maps to Void distance on the cord (S40) with the zones as bands on it; the Whisp still never opens panic. Consequence for story: the fear zone is never a story target (S74 challenge 1) — the bible should say this in the story chapter, not only in growth.

5. **Is Marath the dark future?** Cosmology §3: "positive/negative future is stake, not coordinate; place 3 is not a timeline one travels to." S66 restored the future as the stake of the whole universe; Claude's connection (b) and S70 challenge 2 place the dark branch's home in Marath (Mara at civilisational scale, its beyond the collapse at full strength). Stefan never confirmed. **Options:** (1) Confirm: the myth's dark branch resonates with Marath as Mara resonates with Marath — stake, still not a place one travels to in time. (2) Leave the dark future unlocated in the topology. **Suggest (1)** — it uses the one mechanism the universe already has (S51's resonance) and gives the "Marath's beyond really is" phrase of S76 its referent; but it is a Stefan call, and the "stake not coordinate" line must survive either way.

6. **Fork or continuum future?** S66 says "two ways the future can take"; S31 reconciled the two places into two poles of one continuum, and S66 challenge 6 says the same probably holds for the future. Unanswered. **Suggest:** state the future the way the places are stated — two poles of one continuum, the good and the dark as centres of gravity, "all in between" — so story never has a binary doom switch (fits S70: no countdown). Needs Stefan's yes.

7. **Time in the cosmology.** A sent-back Whisp adds a time axis the worlds topology does not have; whether the far side or the beyond of the warm place *is* the future, or the source of the Whisp's wisdom, is unsaid (S66 challenge 7; S59 challenge 4). **Suggest:** the bible says only what S67/S71 say (the future self reaches back; possible, not guaranteed) and marks the time axis Open; no place in the topology is "the future" until a session says so.

8. **"Home base" — one object or two?** Narrative §Respawn: home base = a safe return point within the arc (S21). Cosmology / roles: the ball is "home base, seed-source, anchor-root" (S39), always one step away (S33), and the private home lies behind it. The Session 01 threads on whether the journey-scoped home base is the same object as the FIM's persistent home were tagged PARTIAL → fundamentals. **Options:** (1) One object: the ball/private home is every story's home base. (2) Two: a story may define its own in-arc home base (a campfire in the episode) distinct from the FIM's home. **Suggest (2)** as the bible's wording ("a story's home base, which may be the FIM's own home"), since S21 speaks of home base *within* the episode and Candidate A's Book-as-portal already uses it that way; mark the identity question Open.

9. **Knowledge tiers — adopt now?** The deep bible / story bible / breadcrumbs / breadcrumb register, mythology arcs vs free episodes, answer-before-the-first-clue, reveal-gated-by-growth were Claude's suggestion (S75). S76 locked where the backdrop lives and says the tiers "follow from this ruling"; the backlog row says the tier and mythology-arc structure "can be settled now (governance, not experience design)"; CQ-018 carries Claude's recommendation (a function inside Universe Studio, not a role; deep bible by commission; a mythology arc is one that touches a fixed point or places a breadcrumb; the repository is not the vault). **Suggest:** adopt the *structure* (three tiers; mythology arcs vs free episodes; a breadcrumb register; the answer exists before the first clue; the personal reveal is gated by growth) as settled in the story chapter, since S76's two-layer ruling cannot be operated without it; leave *who* holds the deep bible and *how* an author enters it to CQ-018 (Open box). Stefan must say yes to the structure explicitly — today it is an approved recommendation, not a statement.

10. **The Held-carries-the-content pattern in Session 03.** S69, S71, S72, S74, S76 are one-line Statements ("confirm", "yes it matches", "yes that works") approving Claude's text. The brief says the Statement wins where they differ; here the Statement has no content of its own. **Suggest:** treat the approved Held as settled (the bible's A8–A9 does so) but have Stefan re-read the specific sentences the bible will carry from them — in particular S76's "never delivered in the experience — no onboarding line, no Whisp line, no screen" and S74's zone-to-ITC table — before they are printed as true.

11. **"The Whisp borrows no authority from the backstory."** A rule Claude held under S69's challenge; Stefan did not state it. It is entailed by S69 (the saviour is the human) and PRINCIPLES-AI. **Suggest:** adopt as a rule of the Whisp's voice, cited to S69 with the note that it was ruled at the bible pass.

12. **The bible is itself deep-bible material.** S76 keeps the myth from the *experience* and the *story bible*, not from a reader of design files; CQ-018 part 4 and CQ-019 say the repository is private and must be split before outside contributors arrive. The Universe Bible will contain A8 in full. **Suggest:** the bible carries a one-line notice that its backdrop chapter is deep-bible tier (never surfaced in the experience, omitted from the story bible), and that its own distribution follows CQ-019. Without this the bible contradicts S75's "keep the authors on their toes".

13. **Respawn nesting "season-repeat" is Claude's extrapolation ratified into canon.** S21 names home base and episode-repeat "just two examples"; the Held added "plausibly season-repeat", and the narrative core prints "episode/season repeat". No contradiction, but the bible should cite the season level as "implied by S21's 'the unit scales with the failure'", not as stated.

14. **The Hero's Journey as frame.** Constitutional (VISION), so it binds — yet no session statement mentions it, Session 01 started "clean", the narrative core does not carry it, and the closest discovery material (S76 lineage: Lucas / Campbell) uses it only to illustrate "knowing why is not being told the secret". **Suggest:** the bible states it once, as VISION does, and adds nothing until a session says how the Hero's Journey relates to seasons/episodes, the Mist's arrival and the graduation arc. Do not elaborate the twelve stages from memory.

15. **"Season Zero" collides with "season".** The community core (Extracted, April 2026) calls the Kickstarter campaign "Season Zero" and its updates "the first episodes of the world"; the narrative core's "season" is an authored unit of story. **Suggest:** keep "Season Zero" as the launch's name with a one-line note that it is a founding moment, not a season in the Arc Studio sense; or rule that the campaign *is* the first authored season (then the story chapter owns it). Community chapter to co-own.

16. **Whose death?** S19 says "the Whisp and its human 'get into trouble and die'"; S22 refines that it is the Whisp that dies and re-enters, the FIM holds continuity. The narrative core already prints the S22 reading. No ruling needed; noted so the bible does not quote S19 literally.

17. **Platform-authored vs FIM-authored narrative.** S13/S29 make Tellers the authors; whether platform-authored (founder/canon-keeper) content also exists was tagged PARTIAL → narrative planned pages, CQ-003. S75–S76 now imply it does (mythology arcs pass through the canon keepers; the founder is the first showrunner per CQ-018). **Suggest:** state that all story is authored by FIMs in Dreamineer mode (S29: nobody builds from outside), that the founder is a FIM, and that mythology arcs are commissioned through the canon function — which answers the old thread without a new statement. Stefan to confirm.

18. **Candidate B item A ("AGI as implementation") against the backdrop.** Candidate B has the Gimbal as ancient knowledge finally implemented by A(G)I; Session 03 has AI as the fork that the Whisp's growth-work answers. Session 03 Open threads route this to the bible's rulings list. **Options:** (1) Drop item A. (2) Keep as candidate: AI closed the gap to the Gimbal *and* is the fork — the same force, two faces, which fits S69's "the tool that could run away with the future is used to hear one's own wiser voice". **Suggest (2) as a candidate to keep open**, not to adopt; it is Gimbal-origin material and belongs to the worlds/beings chapters' rulings, flagged here because it colours the myth.

### C. Candidates touching this chapter

1. **Candidate A #6 — the Book-as-portal**: a Teller's published season or episode is entered as a book found in the village library; closing it respawns at home base; a Library of journeys beside the Tree. No conflict (uses S21 home base, S26 village). **Keep open** — a texture the studios can test; not needed for a true sentence now.
2. **Candidate A #7 — the Mask**: an episode entered by donning a mask that pre-loads the story's discipline (silence, etiquette). No conflict. **Keep open.**
3. **Candidate A #8 — the Cyclone / storm-front**: a rare scheduled event in which the Shimmer reaches for FIMs and those caught share a story — the universe's way of doing collective, simultaneous, ARG-style mass events without breaking voluntariness. Tension: "involuntary in the immediate sense" against S5 voluntariness and S70/S73 (the world never drives a FIM's pace). **Keep open, with the condition** that entry stays opt-in at the festival/weather-window level, as the candidate itself says.
4. **Candidate A #5 — the Mirror-overlay / "Shadow-side"**: the near side has a dark face at thin-Shimmer locations, flipped by gesture, so Tellers need not push every dark story into the far side. Now largely realised by S32 and S52 (Marath's near side is seen through the Gimbal at the same coordinates). Vocabulary conflict: "Shadow-side" was coined against the old Shadow-as-entrant; today's Shadow is Mara's form. **Drop the name; the substance is settled** by S32/S52 (cite those, not the candidate).
5. **Candidate A #10 — the Living-bridge**: a portal that exists only between aligned FIMs, unlocking a shared sub-far-side no one reaches alone — the 1+1 / 1+community journey's signature topology. No conflict; strong fit with S27/S36 and the developmental-honesty rule. **Keep open** (worlds chapter co-owns).
6. **Candidate A #4 — the Anchored descent**: a roped descent using the cord/anchor mechanic, with a "don't look back" in-narrative discipline as a Teller's option. No conflict. **Keep open.**
7. **Candidate A cross-cutting note — every portal type carries one rule or taboo** ("what turns a doorway into a story"); the cord-anchoring discipline already qualifies. **Keep open** as a Teller/Wayfinder craft heuristic; not canon.
8. **Candidate B item F — the first opening and the shutdown of unknown cause** ("a wound… the archives are fragments from before the shutdown") and **item G — the system was designed for place 2 without knowing about place 3** ("the secret at the centre… the likely spine of the novel"). These are a *second* mythology, older than S66's, with its own secret and its own "some FIMs will land in place 3, the Whisp does not know it". Conflicts: G's Whisp that is naive about Marath vs S54/S57/S61 (the Whisp is born full of wisdom and is precisely what meets the Shadow); G's "what determines which face receives you was never understood" vs S52/S55 (both near sides visible through the Gimbal, the far sides reached by anchoring at chosen risk). **Suggest: keep open for the novel, rule out as universe backdrop** unless Stefan wants two mythologies layered (a lost-programme myth under the future-self myth) — then it needs a session, and CQ-018's fixed-point list grows.
9. **Candidate B item A — ancient knowledge, AGI as implementation.** See B18. **Keep open** (Gimbal origin).
10. **Candidate B item E — the three altered states (sleep paralysis in the Shimmer) and OBE landing in Marath.** Session 03 flags these against Mara/the Shadow in Marath (S51, S53) and against "the Whisp travels, the FIM stays body-side" (S61). Story-relevant only as texture for how a crossing feels. **Keep open; beings/worlds chapters own the ruling.**
11. **Candidate C — the name of place 2.** Touches story only as the name a newcomer first hears beside "Marath" and as the S78 "rest" brief (availability, not occupancy). **Keep open**; Stefan names (S65).
12. **Kegan report §4.3 / synthesis 6 — "respawning as immunity intelligence"**: the respawn could surface "here is what the encounter looked like from outside; what might have been at stake?" **Adopted in substance** by S56 (Marath as the testing ground) and S74 (learning zone = the test, with respawn). *Not* adopted: the report's suggestion of a diagnostic prompt at respawn (VoJ/VoC/VoF classification), which would surface theme at the surface (principle 2) and label the FIM. **Adopt the mechanism, drop the surfaced diagnostic.**
13. **Kegan report §4.1 — the orders as a compass for journey design; §4.6 — perspectives and orders.** Not adopted; S58's refinement forbids the platform ever labelling a FIM's order, and CQ-011 flags "NPC calibration to growth zones" as unvalidated. **Keep as research.**
14. **Thinkers report — "The Three-Level Shadow" (individual / pair / community Shadow; the lost travellers' unintegrated material in the collective Shadow).** Partly superseded: S53 and S62 make the Shadow one per FIM, visible along a shared branch — no pair or community Shadow is stated. The "government programme's lost travellers" depends on Candidate B F/G. **Keep open** as a hypothesis for Marath's "other creatures" (S53 says Marath holds more than Shadows); cite nothing from it as true.
15. **Thinkers report — McAdams' narrative identity (redemption vs contamination sequences, nuclear scenes).** Not adopted by any statement; relevant to how a FIM's own story is told back. **Keep as research.**
16. **Thinkers report — Loewenstein's information-gap curiosity** (curiosity lives in the middle band; needs a foothold; deprivation vs interest curiosity). Not adopted by statement, but S70's "something needs to itch" and S55/S76's kept-unknowns are the same design move. **Keep as research; usable as the rationale line under "curiosity as a design principle" if Stefan wants a citation, with the "informed by, never equals" discipline (S28).**
17. **The MANIFESTO value pair "The long view over short-term gain"** — offered under S77, not adopted. **Keep available.**
18. **Trained loops vs discovered loops** (S20, Claude's inference): the FIM knowingly enters a repeating story vs realises mid-story they are in one; possibly a developmental signal (early FIMs get trained loops with clear rules). **Keep open** for the Tellers' craft page.
19. **Candidate A's I Love Bees note** (a fractured AI from the future; payphones at GPS coordinates) is only a lineage example in S23 and in S66 challenge 5 (the ARG pattern as a way to hide the backdrop). **Not adopted** as a mechanic; S76 chose "discovered in the world, kept for a while", which is compatible with but does not commit to ARG delivery. Nothing to adopt beyond A11's lineage line.

### D. Open items — what the bible must mark as Open

1. **The first hour.** What does a newcomer actually do, see and meet in their first hour — the founding narrative, the first mystery and conflict, how the world comes to life the first time, and at what grain a Mist's journey runs? Sources: CQ-010; backlog row "The first hour / first experience"; narrative core §Planned "The first experience"; record §Narrative (mechanics built, narrative unwritten); CQ-014 (the taster journey, the garden glimpse — product surface); ADR-U045's deferred Mist journey-grain question. Waits for: the universe-mechanics fundamentals session (backlog row 1), then a dedicated first-hour discovery session, then specification. Merged: CQ-010 + backlog + narrative core planned page + record gap 1 + CQ-014's story part.

2. **Narrative beyond respawn.** What are seasons and episodes as universe canon — their cadence, their shape, an A-plot/B-story structure or not, personalisation per FIM; what are journeys' route types and content families as creative description? Sources: backlog row "Narrative beyond respawn"; narrative core §Planned (two pages); graduation tracker note "not yet graduated"; B1, B2. Waits for: a discovery session (not scheduled).

3. **Narrative governance at scale — who holds the mythology.** Who are the canon keepers after the founder; how an author moves from the story bible to the deep bible (by growing into it or by appointment); which seasons count as mythology arcs (the fixed-point list); where the deep bible lives. Sources: CQ-018 (with Claude's principles recommendation); backlog row "Narrative governance at scale"; S75 challenge 2; Session 03 open threads. Waits for: a Stefan ruling on the principles (now) and on the mechanics once CQ-004 (Council) and Dreamineers exist. Related: CQ-019 (repository split before contributors).

4. **The reveal design.** Which breadcrumbs, seeded where and when; how the Whisp's filling unlocks the personal reveal; how a mythology arc pays off a clue without dating the world to the 2020s. Sources: S75 point 5; S76 consequence 3; Session 03 open threads. Waits for: the fundamentals, then experience design (2026-06-14 rule).

5. **Marath as the dark future; fork vs continuum; time in the cosmology.** Is Marath's beyond the collapse at full strength; is the future two poles of one continuum; is time an axis, and is any reach of the warm place "the future" or the source of the Whisp's wisdom? Sources: S66 challenges 6–7 and connection (b); S59 challenge 4; S70 challenge 2; Session 03 open threads (three bullets). Waits for: a Stefan decision (B5–B7) or a session. Merged: three Session 03 open threads.

6. **How Dreamineers manifest within the narrative.** Do authors appear inside the world they write (an in-world heteronym like Akerman's Rocco Rivarossi; a Teller as a character), or only behind it? Sources: beings core §Where the creative roles went ("remains an open design thread"); Session 01 open thread on in-world authored characters (tagged OPEN → planned NPC page); S25. Waits for: a discovery session; the studios.

7. **Respawn phenomenology and control.** How in-story death and rewind actually feel (hard cut, slow dissolve, waking at the checkpoint — neither clinical nor traumatic); whether the rewind is FIM-initiated, Whisp-offered or both; whether a FIM can respawn deliberately to try another approach; how the loop's unit is communicated (countdown, dramatic cue, learned through play); how nested respawn levels are surfaced; whether the Whisp behaves differently at home base. Sources: S19 observations; Session 01 open threads (six bullets, tagged PARTIAL/OPEN → planned Whisp page, narrative planned pages, DS-3). Waits for: the narrative session + the Whisp's full specification. Merged: six Session 01 threads.

8. **Group respawn outside the round.** How a pair or group handles individual readiness vs group readiness to retry in a slower, non-round-bounded episode — voting, waiting, Whisp-mediated. Sources: S19, S20, S21 observations; Session 01 open threads (three bullets, tagged PARTIAL/OPEN → narrative planned pages). Waits for: the narrative session. Merged: three threads.

9. **What persists and what it costs.** Per topology and per story: story state resets, awareness / the Whisp's memory / growth persist (S19, S20 — "varies per story"); whether respawn modes other than the cord's two-tier severance carry a cost. Sources: Session 01 threads (tagged PARTIAL → narrative core + cosmology §8; "other modes open"). Waits for: the narrative session; Tellers' craft.

10. **Loop textures and developmental purpose.** Whether a texture (combat / mystery / inquiry / grief) maps to a developmental purpose; trained vs discovered loops and whether the loop's framing is itself a developmental signal. Sources: S20 inference; Session 01 threads (tagged RESOLVED for the palette, "developmental mapping still open"; OPEN for trained/discovered). Waits for: the narrative session.

11. **Home base as an object.** Whether a story's home base is the FIM's ball/private home or a story-defined place; see B8. Sources: S21 observations; Session 01 threads (PARTIAL → fundamentals). Waits for: the fundamentals session.

12. **Wayfinder difficulty levers.** Whether anchor-seed density (sparse = committing, rich = forgiving) is a lever Wayfinders tune; whether specific journeys are gated to require 1+1 or 1+community. Sources: S27 observation (OPEN → planned journeys page); how-growth-works "developmental honesty" thread (PARTIAL → Journey Studio). Waits for: the journeys page; Journey Studio.

13. **The dark pole in craft.** How reality-fiction blur is preserved without ever deceiving a FIM harmfully — the line between immersive ambiguity and genuine confusion is "a design discipline"; the fear zone must never be a story's target. Sources: Session 01 thread (PARTIAL → PRINCIPLES-AI + narrative meta-safety); S70, S74 challenge 1. Waits for: the story bible / Tellers' guidance (governance), not canon.

14. **Which ARG mechanics, if any.** Location-anchored clues, collaborative puzzle-solving, real-world artifacts, distributed media, in-fiction characters that respond in real time; whether collective mysteries are ecosystem-wide or scoped to a season; whether NPCs serve as the responding characters (CQ-013). Sources: S23, S24; Session 01 threads (three bullets, OPEN/PARTIAL → narrative planned pages, CQ-013). Waits for: the narrative session; the studios; CQ-013. Merged: three threads.

15. **NPCs across arcs.** Whether story-critical NPCs persist across seasons or are story-scoped; whether Anthropologists ever author named individuals; the inter-studio protocol for deepening an NPC mid-episode; whether behaviour is a fourth layer. Sources: S30 open seam; Session 01 threads (PARTIAL/OPEN → planned NPC page; CQ-013). Waits for: the NPC page; CQ-013. (Beings chapter co-owns.)

16. **Sensory layering as a craft discipline.** How Tellers and Wayfinders design content against the five-senses requirement. Source: Session 01 thread (OPEN → the studios). Waits for: the studios' DESCRIPTIONs.

17. **Zones per assumption.** A FIM is in a zone per Big Assumption, not as a whole; what, if anything, the Whisp shows of this (no single zone meter). Sources: S74 challenge 2; Session 03 open threads. Waits for: the Whisp's full specification. (Growth chapter owns; story chapter cites the "no zone meter" consequence.)

18. **Rest as a mechanic.** Whether rest carries no cost in the world (no streaks, no decay, no "your village is wilting"); who decides the storm has passed (the FIM, with the Whisp asking "is it still raining, or is it Mara?"); the candidate rule "rest is the Whisp's when chosen while seeing and ending in flight; Mara's when it is avoidance without end". Sources: S78 held and challenges 1–3 (offered, not ruled). Waits for: a Stefan statement.

19. **The Hero's Journey's relation to the universe's own arcs.** See B14. Source: VISION §What this is; no discovery statement. Waits for: a session, if ever.

20. **The novel's mythology (Candidate B F/G) vs the universe's backdrop.** Whether the lost-programme myth exists in the universe at all. Sources: Candidate B items F, G, status summary; Session 03 open threads (Candidate B reconciliations). Waits for: a Stefan ruling (B18, C8).

21. **AI feasibility for adaptive narrative.** Adaptive season personalisation per member, NPC calibration to zones, AI-generative journeys — none validated against what AI can deliver at the quality required. Source: CQ-011. Waits for: build / research. (Listed because the story chapter must not claim adaptive seasons as true — B1.)
